There will be a roll-call vote in principle on Articles 3, 15, 32, 34, 36 and on Amendment Nos 1177 and a special 119 of the draft law of the Ministry of Education, Research and Religious Affairs: ‘Organisation and functioning of higher education, rules on research and other provisions’. The acceptance of the principle, the articles and the amendment of the draft law are 'WHEN'. Not accepting the principle, the articles and the amendment of the bill are 'OH'. The voters say 'PARON'. I would also like to inform you that a fax (fax) has come to the Bureau in accordance with Rule 70a of the Rules of Procedure, which gives its vote on the principle, the articles and the amendment to the draft law. This vote will be announced and taken into account in the count, which will be followed. After the reading is completed, at the time of the count, we will vote on the articles so that we can get time. Colleagues will do the count, and I will do the articles with the rapporteurs so that we can get some time and start the next bill as soon as possible. Mrs Anastasia Gara is on the list by SYRIZA and Mr Vassilis Yoyakas from New Democracy.

'PARON' were voted 20 MPs. Article 15 was therefore accepted, as amended by the Minister, by a majority vote. Article 32 of the draft law: In favour of Rule 32, i.e. 'NAH', they voted 149 Members. Against Rule 32, i.e. 'Notice', they voted 104 Members. "PARON" have voted 5 MPs. Article 32 was therefore accepted, as amended by the Minister, by a majority vote. Article 34 of the draft law: In favour of Rule 34, i.e. 'NAH', they voted 163 MPs. Against Rule 34, i.e. 'OH', they voted 74 Members. 'PARON' they voted 21 MPs. Article 34 was therefore accepted, as amended by the Minister, by a majority. Article 36 of the draft law: In favour of Rule 36, i.e. 'NAH', they voted 149 Members. Against Rule 36, i.e. 'Notice', they voted 104 Members. "PARON" have voted 5 MPs. Article 36 was therefore accepted, as amended by the Minister, by a majority vote. The amendment under number 1177 and a special 119 of the draft law: In favour of the amendment, i.e. 'NAH', they voted 149 Members. On the amendment, i.e. 'Notice', they voted 104 Members. "PARON" have voted 5 MPs. The amendment under number 1177 and a special 119, as amended by the Minister, was therefore accepted by a majority vote.

ParlaMint-GR_2017-08-02-S1-commons.u958	Ladies and gentlemen, the dialogue in the Standing Committee on Social Affairs, which included consultation with thirty-two bodies invited to present comments, has led us to highlight three major issues. First theme: Was the time of political and social dialogue sufficient for a reform that the government considers to be an integral part of its objectives? Secondly, what is the nature of the reform? Is it a state-run, Soviet, disastrous, useless, warring party army, as New Democracy and Golden Dawn claims, or is it favourable to market forces, as the JRC claims? Is it simply an anachronistic, inappropriate, in modern data, a report of ideas without guaranteed funding, as the PASOK representatives claimed? Does the family doctor need a health team with nurses, health visitors, social workers? Do the TOMYs need it? [[At this point, the Second Vice-President of Parliament, Mr GEORGIOS VARENTIS takes over]] Third question: Does the draft law withdraw? With what vision does this bill come down to Parliament, which is adapted to what the Greek Constitution demands? The state to defend the health of its citizens as their fundamental right. Do we have to adapt to the final result of the legislation or to the perception of equal distances from the public and private sectors, which New Democracy presents? Are you here, ladies and gentlemen, about the issues that bring the conflict between the two worlds and about the issues we are talking about and ask an additional question: Are you with the Obama Health Plan or the abolition that Trump wants? Firstly, the dialogue on an integrated primary health care system with a family doctor and a health team, reflecting the positions of the Alma Ata Synod of 1978 on a shift of national health systems to disease prevention, treatment and health promotion, the generalisation of vaccinations, early diagnosis and treatment, is part of the 'Doxiadis draft' and the preparation of the founding law on the ESS 1397/1983. Paraskevi and Giorgos Gennimatas had a thorough discussion on the report and the articles of the draft law, in which I also participated as the Bureau of the Association of Doctors of Northern Greece and Thessaly. The rapporteur's report - I will turn it over to the Minutes - mentioned the fundamental principles of the then PASOK: 'Health is a social asset, which does not obey the laws of profit. Every citizen, regardless of his or her social and economic position and place of residence, has the same right to equal and high-level care and social care. Health protection is an exclusive responsibility of the State. This is also in line with the Greek Constitution's requirement in Article 21. The state's sole responsibility is through a decentralised and democratic health system, which ensures that every citizen's right is fundamentalised, as well as the possibility of continuously improving the levels of health services provided and exploits the achievements and developments of science. This is the concept of the National Health System." These were the intentions of PASOK’s health ministers, proclamations and illusions at that time. And the question at today's PASOK, but also at DIMAR, is this: What position do you, ladies and gentlemen, take with regard to these issues that the draft law raises with the reform? Do you think the concepts of the founding law were anachronistic, state-run, old-fashioned, backward? Study the references of the report on the organisation of primary health care, the need for full and exclusive employment, for health regions, for PSYs, for a family doctor who creates a relationship of trust and interdependence with his patients, for the specific training he needs to have. I'm filing it in the Minutes. Study it. [[At this point the Member, Mr Athanasios Papadopoulos, presents for the Minutes the above-mentioned report, which is in the archives of the Secretariat of the Department of Stenography and Practice of the Parliament.]] Study, ladies and gentlemen, and what comes next in this House. Everyone we've worked in hospitals knows what an emergency department means. In addition, this department will also cut off the umbilical cord that connects the relationship, let me say it in the simplest way, doctor-patient, but also a dependent relationship to the introduction of the patient into the hospital through the emergency, emergencies. The Emergency Department, the autonomous, operating in every major hospital, will cut this umbilical cord too. ParlaMint-GR_2017-08-02-S1-commons.u1138	Mrs Karasarlidou has the floor from SYRIZA.
ParlaMint-GR_2017-08-02-S1-commons.u1139	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, in the past few years we have experienced, in a traumatic way, the side effects of the neoliberal policies implemented, particularly in the field of public health. But today we are discussing a draft law which is a very serious reform in primary health care. It is clear to us that we are carrying out a different political project, aimed at universal coverage of the population, but with a clear view to the public health system. Our aim is equality and quality in care. In this direction was the legislation on universal health insurance for people who are both insecure and insecure and migrants and refugees, so that they can address and go to public health structures. With this draft law, however, we are moving one step further: we are deleting the uninsured debts. Over the past two and a half million people have been excluded from the health system for economic reasons. However, sad - and above all criminal - this fact at the expense of public health runs counter to the free hospitals of MPs, former ministers, former Prime Ministers, as was unfortunately revealed in the Committee of Inquiry into Health. Unfortunately, the political options of the previous governments have had serious adverse effects. They caused the closure of almost all primary hospitals, resulting in citizens conspiring to the urgent incidents of public hospitals that they invented on that day for any of their problems. They have also caused the exit of three thousand doctors from the health system and, of course, the support of the private health sector alone. For all of us, however, the SYRIZA Members, health is a basic social asset, it is a fundamental human right, as we have often said and not a commodity sold under market conditions. We believe that we have an obligation to invest in the universal protection of people, health and quality of life, but also in the sustainability of the health and social state. Let us say a little what we have done over the past two years: we have strengthened the National Health System with permanent and subsidiary staff, we have increased the spending limit in public structures and in EEAS, we have legislated on the transparent list of surgery, patient protection offices, zero participation in the pharmaceutical expenditure of citizens in extreme poverty, and compensation for additional diagnostic and preventive tests by EEAS. With this draft law, we extend the National Health System to its base. By reforming primary health care, we legislate on setting up two hundred forty local health units, namely, in seventy-five cities, which will cover the urban population of two million and thirty-three thousand inhabitants. In order to serve the above services, three thousand will be recruited, of which three hundred will be doctors. Local health units are family medicine units with emphasis on preventive medicine, school health, vaccinations, management of chronic patients and continuous and holistic care of each patient. With this draft law by every citizen, whether he is insured or uninsured, he will have his own family doctor, i.e. his own health adviser. The laboratory sector of primary health care is also being upgraded so that it can meet more needs of insured and uninsured citizens. Public dental care is guaranteed. We cover with prescribing the Pap Test and other different pregnancy diagnostic tests, which is a very important adjustment for the female population. Health visitors are valued in order to promote the health of each individual, the family, the community, the school population. The Opposition accuses us that the family doctor will act as a patient cutter to the hospitals and that we are destroying the free choice for each patient. The truth, however, is that the former system created a furniture freedom of choice. In fact, the citizens had to choose either the downgraded public health system - because of the government's conscious choices - or the overcompensated and fragmented private health services. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law of the primary health wheel is a modern and anthropcentric project for the reorganisation and support of the many health sectors in our country. The bet for us is to eliminate inequalities by investing in health as a critical indicator of overall social welfare. These are the reasons why we invite you, we call upon all the political forces to vote for this draft law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2017-08-02-S1-commons.u1201	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, since the first time the'memorandum' has been implemented, the social state, and in particular the health and education sectors have been at the forefront of'memorandum' policies, with the result that expenditure has been cut down, that it has been under-funded, that the anemics in our country are under-represented, but that there are existing structures in the social state. Moreover, the conflicting corruption had also taken care of before a crisis, but even after it had been plundered with resources, the dismantling of public money, and even the divestment of socially beneficial structures of the health system, such as Henry Dynan Hospital, which are already being investigated by the Committee of Inquiry into Health. All this has led to a definitive cancellation of the ESS, i.e. a post-demolition, Mr. Parliament was representing PASOK. SYRIZA, taking responsibility for the governance of the country, made it a priority, in the first phase, to keep the ESS up, i.e. the health of Greek citizens, then revive it and re-establish it on new foundations. Its first speeches are well known and have been mentioned by many colleagues. It has gradually increased, over time, the health budget, despite the well-known conditions of budgetary suffocation. Uninsured people have obtained access to medical care, including examinations. Clinics of the Clinics have increased. Confirmed debts and debts, amounting to EUR 37 million, were cancelled from services offered to uninsured persons and had been transferred to the DU and other debts of EUR 150 million, which were prevented from being transferred. The interventions of the bill are the idea that health is a fundamental human right, a basic social asset and not a commodity for sale with market rules. In this context, the current draft legislation, which concerns primary health care, is part of the national health system. Its arrangements are based on a strategic support strategy for upgrading and reforming the system of primary health care services, recognising the subsidiary complementary role of the private sector. It promotes ensuring the right to free universal, equal, non-discriminatory access to public health units, the transparent and democratic administrative organisation and the e-government of primary health care, the financial protection of citizens for private costs of meeting their health problems, social control and accountability of health institutions. The network of existing structures in primary care remains the second level of services of the primary care system. This network will first be surrounded by another network of decentralised structures and services, regional health centres. In this network, local health units, aimed at a particular population, are managed by an interbranch interdisciplinary health team comprising a family doctor, pediatricians, nurses, health visitors, a social worker. TEMPUS through health groups are developing a new standard in the national health system. They constitute family medicine units with emphasis on preventive medicine, school health, chronic disease management, and citizens' continuous and integrated care. The TOMYs are the first point of contact of the citizen with the National Health System, but at the same time they also develop extroverts and Community interventions (infections in vulnerable groups, information activities for the general population, regular visits to schools, KAPIs and workplaces). This new network is responsible for looking for proper health care for every citizen. Thus, the bill constitutes a change of model for the ESS, since the responsibility for a personal case becomes collective. That is to say, the recovery of the public's health liability from the health system. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] This is also the case with the development of family doctors at TOMY by reorientating the work of general doctors, who are currently serving in health centres and regional doctors, to prevent, treat health care, Community care, and possibly refer them to other levels of the health system, as well as to the development of targeted and complementary new ECHW contracts with family doctors. That's how a health counselor is placed next to every citizen. Evidently, the referral procedure excludes exceptional and urgent circumstances, as set out in paragraph 6 of Article 19 of the draft law. I can't extend to other issues due to time. I would just like to stress that the strengthening of the role of other health professionals involved in primary health care and active participation in it by all health professionals is necessary, because this results in a completely different model of the ESS, in the direction of limiting the health-centric character of the system. Two final remarks concerning the countryside. We know the financial constraints, Minister. The philosophy governing both local health units and family doctors, according to the bill, could be first and gradually transformed into rural health centres and then expanded to them. A second. The EMCDDA is well-known to be an agency operating under the HR's health policy. In this context, it is necessary to establish EAW stations in mountain and remote areas in places to be selected and facilitate the transportation of patients from health centres to the nearest hospital. There must also be associated planning to operate EPC mobile units in order to avoid endangering the lives and health of people living in these areas. To conclude, ladies and gentlemen, the current bill, such as the one which preceded higher education, and the one that will follow, for the work of the audiovisual media, are institutional interventions aimed at reconstituting the social state and establishing the element of transparency in public life. Public health support is done with the help of new decentralised structures, local health units (TOMY), with a focus on the family doctor and the multidisciplinary health team, including general doctors, pathologists, pediatricians, nurses, health visitors, social worker and will provide holistic care to a particular number of citizens, focusing on prevention and health treatment. The TEMPUS are the first point of contact of the citizen with the National Health System. Through local health establishments, they are family medicine units, with emphasis on preventive medicine, with continuous and complete provision of health care to the citizen. EEAY will continue to contribute with private doctors, providing primary health care to their doctors, to meet the required needs. Among the important innovations of the bill are the establishment and operation of each health centre in a dental unit, the upgrading of maternity care in the ESS and the emergence of the necessity of its role in pregnancy, childbirth and issues of gynaecological cancer, the use of health visitors with issues of intervention and action in primary health care. Thus, primary health care contributes to the consolidation and role of the public system by complementing the role of the private sector. The family doctor is essential to their citizen’s health adviser. Over the past few years we have achieved several important changes, such as covering all uninsured fellow citizens with medical care, which has practically sealed our political position that health is a public and universal good. In addition, we have tried to uncover scandals and mismanagement in various health centres and agencies. The Committee of Inquiry also deals with these issues. The incidents that surfaced clearly show that political management in the field of health of the former governments of New Democracy and PASOK was the complete dissolution of public health. There will be an attempt to overcome intrinsic problems of the ESS, which have made the situation much more difficult. This draft law reconstitutes the health system administratively and achieves universal and easier access for the citizen to it. All primary structures, now called health centres, are guaranteed and upgraded in terms of benefits and workshops. In addition, regional local health units, well-known TOMY, are established, which are addressed to a particular population and include a family doctor, preventive medicine, vaccination, school health, and management of chronic patients. In fact, every citizen will have his own family doctor for advice. In health centres there will be a dental team, a midwife, and visitors will be exploited with the aim of informing and preventing. Ladies and gentlemen, this bill introduces a new concept that overthrows the old. Previously, the patient was more informed than a consumer, who has often failed to do the right thing for his health, because he was using health structures on the beam or on the basis of recommendations. The advisory and informational nature of the new primary health structures aims to overthrow this pathogen. The new system strengthens the access of all our fellow citizens to public health and undertakes to advise the citizen on his treatment. Technology plays an important role in this upgrade with the individual electronic health file of the citizen, which will be updated by the family doctor. The operation of primary structures along with the subsidiary role of the private sector will be able to help decongested hospitals, which will be able to respond better to more severe incidents. Successful operation of primary health is a prerequisite for the effectiveness of the whole system. I know from experience since I am active pharmacists. I would like to say, however, to the ministers: they know very well, better than I do, that in many excluded villages in our country many things will not be found, but pharmacies will be found. The pharmacy in these areas of our country is indeed 'driving Thermopylae'. For some time now, it has been offering primary care to our fellow citizens and I would ask that the pharmacy be included in the field of primary health care.

Firstly, we are setting up an effective interministerial body between the two Ministries, Education and Health, which will deal with all the issues involved and have common areas of intervention and policy: the clinical work carried out at university hospitals, research, health professions, professional rights relating to health and other occupations. It's an extensive field. It was a request from old university doctors and doctors of the ESS, so that we do not function as two ministries that do not communicate and interconnect, but that there is a common effort, institutionally planned, to form a single policy. That's the one. The second is this issue, in fact, with the disciplinary framework for university doctors, ICP members, university scholars, who work in university clinics and laboratories, who are part of ERC hospitals. So disciplinary control over their clinical work and misconduct related to the conduct of the clinical work and not other misconduct, such as in the field of education, research, etc. or whatever else, it will be exercised in the same disciplinary framework as for the doctors of the ERC. I think it is indeed an intervention that it needed. It is mature and imperative and we want it to be clear that all doctors, regardless of their employment relationships, are subject to the same accountability and control regime. I'm tabling them in the Minutes.

The first amendment, with a general number of 1202 and a special number 84, concerns the regulation that we extend the term of the subsidiary doctors whose term of office expires on 30-9-2017 and the subsidiary staff serving at the ERC hospitals today, both of them until 31-12-2018. This regulation is necessary, as a large number of recruitments either by doctors or permanent staff has not been fully implemented and the simultaneous withdrawal, especially of subsidiary doctors, on the same date as a serious piece of ancillary staff will create a problem of malfunction. So by giving this extension up to 31/12-2018, we ensure stability in the system. This regulation concerns about a thousand five hundred - a thousand seven hundred workers in the system, either doctors or other staff, and gives the procedure for completing all the notices and occupations, both by permanent doctors and by permanent staff. The second paragraph of this amendment concerns the possibility - for which a criticism has also been made - of the health workers appointed by the AACC '98 competition, who were appointed eighteen years after the adoption of the results of the tender, to be transferred to hospitals or healthcare structures near the place where they had built their lives for eighteen years while they had been deemed to be appointed. This process has been legitimised and it has already been done. This is about nine hundred and fifty-four people. We believe that around two hundred with three hundred, based on the demands we have, will request a transfer to another area than the one in which they were appointed. This process has been legalised and is already made for the other ones by the '98 SAEP competition, which were announced for other structures in the public sector. It's nothing new. It's happening there too. It is simply because the health notice and distribution were made before the other sectors, that was not the case. The second amendment, with a general number of 1203 and a special number 85, is the amendment on which the request for unconstitutionality was tabled and which I think I explained in detail before, in my reply to the closure of the debate. It concerns the introduction of the unpaid post to the president and the Vice-President of the CEDEFOP, although we have in this way legislated for people who have blocked the procedure by means of judicial decision-making, and we are giving extensions of this kind monthly, two months or three months, for existing contracts with contractors, again some of whom do not sign the orders to pay those expenses, so that this staff can be paid. The main thing is to repeat the arrangement for rebate of the volume discount, made to the various providers of the EEAS, in which, by mistake, the ellipse publication of the ellipse in the FEI entered the elbow elsewhere, and in fact the type of discount cannot be calculated in the right way. So we unite all the rebates in one with a particular type, which deals with expenditure on pharmaceuticals, but also with providers and, in fact, we are recasting the provision in order to publish it properly. Secondly, it provides the possibility of offsetting outstanding debts with due rebate or clawback from current uses, which facilitates the liquidity of the EERC providers. It also solves issues relating to the EEA Legal Service, which was supposed to be established, but has never worked, and issues that are related to the cover of its insured agencies and the possibility of being associated with insurance agencies from other countries, to cover Greek counterparts who work there and pay insurance funds in Greece in order to cover their hospitality in those countries where they live. These are in particular the provisions contained in these amendments. Also, now that the EEA has extended the procurement procedure and medical devices with all providers and pharmacies, etc., it provides some transitional provisions in order to unravel the old product, which were more increased, compared to what is now given and covered by the organization at lower prices, on the basis of existing contracts. These are the three amendments which we supported in the committee, but which were tabled late, after the end of the sitting.

ParlaMint-GR_2017-08-02-S1-commons.u1460	Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law we are debating today is indeed innovative because it once again marks the essential restoration of the national health system, firstly because it focuses on the primary level of services, that is to say, the threshold of health services and, secondly, because it sets out the terms and framework for the prevention and health care framework, indivisible parts of public health. Also, after thirty-four years and the founding law of the ESS, the draft law we are discussing is, we could say, the institutional integration of the National Health System, because it broadens its base with the establishment of primary health care. It forms it, that is, the main role in the public system, it guarantees health as a public asset which the state defends, and which ensures equal access to everyone and to all of them, whether they are insured or not. In other words, colleagues and fellow Members, the government is innovating in the context of existing European policies and the World Health Organisation and in the context of the extremely narrow financial possibilities imposed by the country's austerity and comitology. Despite difficult circumstances, it in practice enshrines an unnegotiable right, such as the right to health, the promotion of health services. In fact, despite what has been said, and on the part of the Opposition, to finance the government's specific flagship initiative, the funding, colleagues and colleagues, is absolutely guaranteed. And as the Minister has said, it is guaranteed through the reform programme of the public system, as well as the axis to combat poverty and social exclusion. Indeed, it ensures not only the full utilization of the capacity and the ability of the public health system, but also the complementary and subsidiary role of the private sector, that is, where the ESS really needs it and not as a basic provider, as the New Democracy wants to have the private sector. But let us look more specifically at what this national health system is predicting and how it is structured. I will only refer to the first level of local health units, which are decentralised structures, so that is what we say the heart of the decentralised system, the public health system. Local health establishments are a family medical unit - it is the first contact point for citizens - but at the same time they are developing extroverted and Community interventions - and this is very important to mention - because they are organizing regular visits to schools, to CFPs, to information actions to the general population. And in all respects we could say that they are indeed creating a flexible example that is not relevant to existing and outdated perceptions of one-dimensional medical health. Instead, it addresses health issues within the social, cultural, political, local context, and from this point of view, I would venture to say that it will contribute in the long term to the relativisation of prevention, the concept of the biological body as a universal, stable and political neutral entity, apart from history, language and geography, as is often the case in the field of biomedical sciences, as Athena Athanasios said in her study. Indicative, too, is in the context of this modern example that the general perception and responsibility for care is changed, which from individual level to primary health care network and the National Health System. At the same time, in this context, I consider the concept of public accountability and social control of primary care units, which, for the first time, were imported, are of enormous importance for transparency, but also for socialisation, I would say, of the role of health. With the draft law, which we are debating today, we are seeking all citizens, men and women, to have their own family doctor, their own or their own health adviser, the person responsible for monitoring and managing health problems, to keep an individual electronic record, for their holistic and quality care. In fact, particularly with regard to the responsibilities and responsibilities of the family doctor, I would like to stress that, with regard to the adult population, including, it also contributes to the social well-being of citizens and the general public in a particular area, and I consider this to be extremely important, given that we live in a country in which the importance of psychosocial well-being and the role it plays in health and, in general, social cohesion is often ignored and ignored. With regard to the role of the family doctor for the child and adolescent population, I consider it extremely important to provide for the support of mothers in matters of breast-feeding and for the provision of advisory support for children, adolescents and parents, in matters of sexual health, contraception, addiction, nutritional disorders and care in the family, to prevent mental health problems. I could not mention the establishment of a network of midwives and obstetricians, which will contribute decisively to both prevention and promotion and to addressing serious health problems for women and especially young women. In other words, maternity care in the ESS is upgraded and the advisory and supportive role of midwives in the prevention of gynaecological cancer, pregnancy and childbirth is highlighted. In conclusion, the primary health care system that we want to go through with this draft law focuses on humans, ensures universal access to health services, ensures equality, extends the National Health System. This project aims, not only to regulate relations and competition between the public and private sectors, but to ensure the dominant role of the public system and the complementarity of the private sector. In fact, this decision is a specific ideological political choice and we are not ashamed to say it. It is a specific ideological political choice, based on the established capacity of public systems to respond with greater adequacy of population needs in times of crisis. We therefore take into account the weaknesses of existing structures, but at the same time we seek to shield them with regional structures. We are aware and responding to the identified failure of the existing network of primary health care providers of the EEA, so we are expanding the content, increasing the number of contractors and covering our insecure citizens in areas that do not act on local units. Together with the provision for Article 73, for the cancellation of uninsured debts, we are achieving universal access to the health system. I conclude with something said by the Minister of Health Andreas Xanthos: "Reform in primary health care is the strategic response to the crisis of the health system and its passive privatisation". That is why, ladies and gentlemen, we believe that this draft law actually regenerates primary health. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2017-08-02-S1-commons.u1509	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, what does a engineer have to do with health? But even the late George Gennimatas was an engineer. I have studied the bill and have focused on a number of points, which I consider appropriate to mention today. First of all, I'll mention the target. The aim of the bill is to ensure citizens' right to free access to public health provision units, which in my view is the beneficial, smart choice for society. Secondly, the protection, respect for the burden on taxpayers, of which the costs for the health system are covered. It is precisely that mixture that needs to emerge in conjunction with the private health sector, which will cover some gaps where the public system may be considered more expensive. Thirdly, the prevention that ensures just direct access to the health sector by this bill. All of this is based on the provision of Article 21 of the Constitution, which is directly established - this attributes - the obligation of the state to take positive measures to protect citizens' health, which is at the same time the expression of the principle of social rule of law that we all want in our country and the Constitution of Greece hides the truth. The bill moves in the spirit of an ESS, as Spyridon Doxiadis previously targeted as Minister of Social Services, but his attempt was blocked by the very same circles that today are hosting our efforts. Furthermore, there is a definition of the family doctor and is defined as the appropriate population of responsibility per doctor, both for adults and for children. It is planned to conclude a contract with private individuals, such as medical clubs, the Pan-Hellenic Medical Association or private hospitals, for the purpose of providing medical treatment. The private sector is therefore not excluded from continuing its activities where it can offer. All the above and still provided for in the bill are statistically reflected in order to be rationally managed the resources available always with respect for the leverage of taxpayers. However, where this bill is innovating is the first time in our country of specialization in emergency medicine, i.e. the first-line doctors in emergencies.
